    "Bayesian Network - Burglary Alarm": {
        "prefix": "bayes_burglary",
        "body": [
            "from pgmpy.models import DiscreteBayesianNetwork",
            "from pgmpy.factors.discrete import TabularCPD",
            "from pgmpy.inference import VariableElimination",
            "",
            "# ---- Step 1: Define DAG structure (edges) ----",
            "model = DiscreteBayesianNetwork([",
            "    ('Burglary',   'Alarm'),",
            "    ('Earthquake', 'Alarm'),",
            "    ('Alarm',      'JohnCalls'),",
            "    ('Alarm',      'MaryCalls')",
            "])",
            "",
            "# ---- Step 2: CPDs  (row 0 = False/No, row 1 = True/Yes) ----",
            "cpd_burglary   = TabularCPD('Burglary',   2, [[0.999],[0.001]])",
            "cpd_earthquake = TabularCPD('Earthquake', 2, [[0.998],[0.002]])",
            "",
            "# Columns: B=F,E=F | B=F,E=T | B=T,E=F | B=T,E=T",
            "cpd_alarm = TabularCPD('Alarm', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.999, 0.71, 0.06, 0.05],   # Alarm=False",
            "            [0.001, 0.29, 0.94, 0.95]],   # Alarm=True",
            "    evidence=['Burglary','Earthquake'], evidence_card=[2,2])",
            "",
            "# Columns: Alarm=False | Alarm=True",
            "cpd_john = TabularCPD('JohnCalls', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.3, 0.9],[0.7, 0.1]],",
            "    evidence=['Alarm'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "cpd_mary = TabularCPD('MaryCalls', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.2, 0.99],[0.8, 0.01]],",
            "    evidence=['Alarm'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "model.add_cpds(cpd_burglary, cpd_earthquake, cpd_alarm, cpd_john, cpd_mary)",
            "assert model.check_model(), 'Model is incorrect'",
            "",
            "# ---- Step 3: Variable Elimination inference ----",
            "inference = VariableElimination(model)",
            "",
            "# P(Burglary | JohnCalls=True, MaryCalls=True)",
            "result = inference.query(variables=['Burglary'],",
            "                        evidence={'JohnCalls': 1, 'MaryCalls': 1})",
            "print(result)",
            "",
            "# P(Alarm | Burglary=True)",
            "result2 = inference.query(variables=['Alarm'], evidence={'Burglary': 1})",
            "print(result2)"
        ],
        "description": "Bayesian Network - Burglary Alarm with Variable Elimination"
    },

    "Bayesian Network - Generic Template (Disease/Symptoms)": {
        "prefix": "bayes_disease",
        "body": [
            "from pgmpy.models import DiscreteBayesianNetwork",
            "from pgmpy.factors.discrete import TabularCPD",
            "from pgmpy.inference import VariableElimination",
            "",
            "# Disease -> Fever, Cough, Fatigue, Chills",
            "model = DiscreteBayesianNetwork([",
            "    ('Disease', 'Fever'),",
            "    ('Disease', 'Cough'),",
            "    ('Disease', 'Fatigue'),",
            "    ('Disease', 'Chills')",
            "])",
            "",
            "# P(Disease): 0=Flu, 1=Cold   (variable_card=2 means 2 states)",
            "cpd_disease = TabularCPD('Disease', 2, [[0.3],[0.7]])   # P(Flu)=0.3, P(Cold)=0.7",
            "",
            "# P(Fever | Disease)  — col 0=Flu, col 1=Cold",
            "cpd_fever = TabularCPD('Fever', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.1, 0.5],   # Fever=No",
            "            [0.9, 0.5]],  # Fever=Yes",
            "    evidence=['Disease'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "cpd_cough = TabularCPD('Cough', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.2, 0.4],[0.8, 0.6]],",
            "    evidence=['Disease'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "cpd_fatigue = TabularCPD('Fatigue', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.3, 0.7],[0.7, 0.3]],",
            "    evidence=['Disease'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "cpd_chills = TabularCPD('Chills', 2,",
            "    values=[[0.4, 0.6],[0.6, 0.4]],",
            "    evidence=['Disease'], evidence_card=[2])",
            "",
            "model.add_cpds(cpd_disease, cpd_fever, cpd_cough, cpd_fatigue, cpd_chills)",
            "assert model.check_model()",
            "",
            "inference = VariableElimination(model)",
            "",
            "# Inference Task 1: P(Disease | Fever=Yes, Cough=Yes)",
            "r1 = inference.query(['Disease'], evidence={'Fever':1,'Cough':1})",
            "print('P(Disease | Fever=Yes, Cough=Yes):'); print(r1)",
            "",
            "# Inference Task 2: P(Disease | Fever=Yes, Cough=Yes, Chills=Yes)",
            "r2 = inference.query(['Disease'], evidence={'Fever':1,'Cough':1,'Chills':1})",
            "print('P(Disease | Fever, Cough, Chills=Yes):'); print(r2)",
            "",
            "# Inference Task 3: P(Fatigue | Disease=Flu)",
            "r3 = inference.query(['Fatigue'], evidence={'Disease':0})  # 0=Flu",
            "print('P(Fatigue | Disease=Flu):'); print(r3)"
        ],
        "description": "Bayesian Network - Disease/Symptoms with 3 inference tasks"
    },

    "Markov Chain - Simulation": {
        "prefix": "markov_chain",
        "body": [
            "import numpy as np",
            "",
            "# States and transition matrix",
            "states = ['Red', 'Blue']",
            "transition_matrix = np.array([",
            "    [0.5, 0.5],   # From Red  -> [Red, Blue]",
            "    [0.5, 0.5]    # From Blue -> [Red, Blue]",
            "])",
            "",
            "def simulate_markov_process(initial_state, num_steps):",
            "    current = initial_state",
            "    sequence = [current]",
            "    for _ in range(num_steps):",
            "        idx = states.index(current)",
            "        current = np.random.choice(states, p=transition_matrix[idx])",
            "        sequence.append(current)",
            "    return sequence",
            "",
            "seq = simulate_markov_process('Red', 10)",
            "print('Sequence:', ' -> '.join(seq))"
        ],
        "description": "Markov Chain simulation - marble bag example"
    },

    "Markov Chain - 3-State Weather": {
        "prefix": "markov_weather",
        "body": [
            "import numpy as np",
            "",
            "states = ['Sunny', 'Cloudy', 'Rainy']",
            "transition_matrix = np.array([",
            "    [0.7, 0.2, 0.1],   # From Sunny",
            "    [0.3, 0.4, 0.3],   # From Cloudy",
            "    [0.2, 0.3, 0.5]    # From Rainy",
            "])",
            "",
            "def simulate_weather(initial_state, num_days, seed=42):",
            "    np.random.seed(seed)",
            "    current, seq = initial_state, [initial_state]",
            "    for _ in range(num_days - 1):",
            "        idx = states.index(current)",
            "        current = np.random.choice(states, p=transition_matrix[idx])",
            "        seq.append(current)",
            "    return seq",
            "",
            "weather = simulate_weather('Sunny', 10)",
            "print('10-day forecast:', ' -> '.join(weather))",
            "print('Rainy days:', weather.count('Rainy'))",
            "",
            "# Monte Carlo: P(at least 3 rainy days in 10)",
            "count = sum(1 for _ in range(10000) if simulate_weather('Sunny',10).count('Rainy')>=3)",
            "print(f'P(>=3 rainy days) ≈ {count/10000:.4f}')"
        ],
        "description": "Markov Model - 3-state weather + Monte Carlo probability"
    },

    "Hidden Markov Model - Viterbi": {
        "prefix": "hmm_viterbi",
        "body": [
            "import numpy as np",
            "",
            "# ---- HMM Definition ----",
            "# Hidden states:   'Hot', 'Cold'",
            "# Observations:    0,1,2  (representing 1,2,3 ice creams eaten)",
            "states       = ['Hot', 'Cold']",
            "observations = [0, 1, 2]",
            "",
            "pi = np.array([0.8, 0.2])           # Initial state distribution",
            "A  = np.array([[0.7, 0.3],           # Transition matrix",
            "               [0.4, 0.6]])",
            "B  = np.array([[0.2, 0.4, 0.4],      # Emission matrix: B[state][obs]",
            "               [0.5, 0.4, 0.1]])",
            "",
            "# ---- Viterbi Algorithm ----",
            "def viterbi(obs_seq):",
            "    n_states, T = len(pi), len(obs_seq)",
            "    dp = np.zeros((n_states, T))",
            "    bp = np.zeros((n_states, T), dtype=int)",
            "",
            "    dp[:, 0] = pi * B[:, obs_seq[0]]    # Initialization",
            "",
            "    for t in range(1, T):               # Recursion",
            "        for s in range(n_states):",
            "            prob        = dp[:, t-1] * A[:, s] * B[s, obs_seq[t]]",
            "            dp[s, t]    = np.max(prob)",
            "            bp[s, t]    = np.argmax(prob)",
            "",
            "    path = np.zeros(T, dtype=int)       # Backtrack",
            "    path[-1] = np.argmax(dp[:, -1])",
            "    for t in range(T-2, -1, -1):",
            "        path[t] = bp[path[t+1], t+1]",
            "",
            "    return [states[i] for i in path], np.max(dp[:, -1])",
            "",
            "# ---- Forward Algorithm (probability of observation sequence) ----",
            "def forward(obs_seq):",
            "    n_states, T = len(pi), len(obs_seq)",
            "    alpha = np.zeros((n_states, T))",
            "    alpha[:, 0] = pi * B[:, obs_seq[0]]",
            "    for t in range(1, T):",
            "        for s in range(n_states):",
            "            alpha[s, t] = np.sum(alpha[:, t-1] * A[:, s]) * B[s, obs_seq[t]]",
            "    return np.sum(alpha[:, -1])",
            "",
            "obs_seq = [2, 1, 0, 2]   # Observations",
            "path, prob = viterbi(obs_seq)",
            "print('Observation sequence:', obs_seq)",
            "print('Most likely state sequence:', ' -> '.join(path))",
            "print('Viterbi probability:', prob)",
            "print('Forward probability (P(O|model)):', forward(obs_seq))"
        ],
        "description": "HMM - Viterbi decoding + Forward algorithm from scratch"
    },

    "K-Means Clustering - Elbow Method": {
        "prefix": "kmeans",
        "body": [
            "import numpy as np",
            "import pandas as pd",
            "import matplotlib.pyplot as plt",
            "from sklearn.cluster import KMeans",
            "from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler",
            "",
            "# ---- Load Data ----",
            "df = pd.read_csv('${1:Mall_Customers.csv}')",
            "print(df.head())",
            "",
            "# ---- Extract Features ----",
            "x = df.iloc[:, [3, 4]].values   # Adjust column indices for your dataset",
            "",
            "# ---- Elbow Method: find optimal K ----",
            "# WCSS = Within-Cluster Sum of Squares",
            "# WCSS = Σ (distance of each point to its centroid)^2",
            "wcss_list = []",
            "for i in range(1, 11):",
            "    kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=i, init='k-means++', random_state=42)",
            "    kmeans.fit(x)",
            "    wcss_list.append(kmeans.inertia_)   # inertia_ = WCSS",
            "",
            "plt.plot(range(1,11), wcss_list, marker='o')",
            "plt.title('The Elbow Method Graph')",
            "plt.xlabel('Number of clusters (k)'); plt.ylabel('WCSS')",
            "plt.show()",
            "",
            "# ---- Scale Features (improves clustering) ----",
            "scaler  = StandardScaler()",
            "X_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(x)",
            "",
            "# ---- Train K-Means (choose k from elbow graph) ----",
            "k = 5   # Change based on elbow",
            "kmeans    = KMeans(n_clusters=k, init='k-means++', random_state=42)",
            "y_predict = kmeans.fit_predict(X_scaled)",
            "",
            "# ---- Visualize ----",
            "colors = ['blue','green','red','black','purple','orange','cyan']",
            "for i in range(k):",
            "    plt.scatter(x[y_predict==i,0], x[y_predict==i,1],",
            "                s=100, c=colors[i], label=f'Cluster {i+1}')",
            "plt.scatter(kmeans.cluster_centers_[:,0], kmeans.cluster_centers_[:,1],",
            "            s=300, c='yellow', label='Centroid')",
            "plt.title('Customer Clusters'); plt.xlabel('Feature 1'); plt.ylabel('Feature 2')",
            "plt.legend(); plt.show()",
            "",
            "df['Cluster'] = y_predict",
            "print(df.head())"
        ],
        "description": "K-Means clustering - elbow method, scaling, visualize clusters"
    },

        ],

            "}",

            "",
            "# ---- Feature Selection & Scaling ----",
            "features = ['GPA', 'study_hours', 'attendance_rate']",
            "X        = df[features].values",
            "scaler   = StandardScaler()",

            "    km = KMeans(n_clusters=k, init='k-means++', random_state=42)",
            "    km.fit(X_scaled)",
            "    wcss.append(km.inertia_)",
            "plt.plot(range(2,7), wcss, marker='o')",
            "plt.title('Elbow Method (K=2 to 6)'); plt.xlabel('k'); plt.ylabel('WCSS'); plt.show()",
            "",
            "# ---- Cluster with optimal K ----",
            "optimal_k = 3   # Change based on elbow",
            "kmeans    = KMeans(n_clusters=optimal_k, init='k-means++', random_state=42)",
            "df['Cluster'] = kmeans.fit_predict(X_scaled)",
            "",
            "# ---- Scatter Plot (study_hours vs GPA) ----",
            "colors = ['blue','green','red','purple','orange']",
            "for c in range(optimal_k):",
            "    sub = df[df['Cluster']==c]",
            "    plt.scatter(sub['study_hours'], sub['GPA'], c=colors[c], label=f'Cluster {c}', s=80)",
            "plt.title(f'Student Clusters (k={optimal_k})')",
            "plt.xlabel('Study Hours'); plt.ylabel('GPA'); plt.legend(); plt.show()",
            "",
            "print(df[['student_id','GPA','study_hours','attendance_rate','Cluster']])"
        ],

"theory": {
  "prefix": "theory",
  "body": [
    "This is a comprehensive breakdown of the core concepts, examiner-style questions, and essential code snippets for Labs 07 through 11. I’ve distilled these into \"universal\" principles that apply regardless of the specific dataset or scenario used.",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "## Lab 07: Constraint Satisfaction Problems (CSP)",
    "**Core Concept:** Solving problems by identifying a set of variables that must satisfy a set of constraints or limitations.",
    "",
    "* **Q: What are the three components of a CSP?**",
    "    * **A:** Variables ($V$), Domains ($D$), and Constraints ($C$).",
    "* **Q: Explain Backtracking Search in CSP.**",
    "    * **A:** It is a depth-first search that chooses values for one variable at a time and \"backtracks\" when a variable has no legal values left to assign.",
    "* **Q: What is the purpose of the AC-3 algorithm?**",
    "    * **A:** It enforces **Arc Consistency**, reducing the domain sizes of variables before or during search to speed up the process.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "A state is consistent if it does not violate any constraints:",
    "$$\\forall c \\in C, \\text{is\\_satisfied}(c, \\text{assignment})$$",
    "",
    "**Essential Code Snippet:**",
    "```python",
    "from constraint import Problem",
    "problem = Problem()",
    "problem.addVariable(\"A\", [1, 2, 3]) # Variable and Domain",
    "problem.addConstraint(lambda a, b: a != b, (\"A\", \"B\")) # Constraint",
    "solutions = problem.getSolutions()",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "## Lab 08: Adversarial Search (Minimax & Pruning)",
    "**Core Concept:** Decision-making in competitive \"zero-sum\" environments (like Chess or Tic-Tac-Toe).",
    "",
    "* **Q: How does the Minimax algorithm work?**",
    "    * **A:** The **MAX** player tries to maximize the score, while the **MIN** player tries to minimize it. The algorithm recursively explores the game tree to find the optimal move.",
    "* **Q: What is Alpha-Beta Pruning?**",
    "    * **A:** An optimization technique for Minimax that stops evaluating a branch as soon as it's proven to be worse than a previously examined branch.",
    "* **Q: What are $\\alpha$ and $\\beta$ values?**",
    "    * **A:** $\\alpha$ is the best value MAX can guarantee; $\\beta$ is the best value MIN can guarantee.",
    "",
    "**Universal Logic:**",
    "$$\\text{Prune if } \\alpha \\geq \\beta$$",
    "",
    "**Essential Code Snippet:**",
    "```python",
    "def minimax(node, depth, is_max, alpha, beta):",
    "    if depth == 0 or node.is_terminal(): return node.value",
    "    if is_max:",
    "        best = -float('inf')",
    "        for child in node.children:",
    "            best = max(best, minimax(child, depth-1, False, alpha, beta))",
    "            alpha = max(alpha, best)",
    "            if beta <= alpha: break # Pruning",
    "        return best",
    "    # (Similar logic for MIN using min() and beta)",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "## Lab 09: Bayesian Networks & Markov Models",
    "**Core Concept:** Handling uncertainty using probability and tracking sequences of events.",
    "",
    "* **Q: What is a Bayesian Network?**",
    "    * **A:** A Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) representing variables and their conditional dependencies.",
    "* **Q: State Bayes' Theorem.**",
    "    * **A:** It calculates posterior probability based on prior knowledge:",
    "    $$P(A|B) = \\frac{P(B|A)P(A)}{P(B)}$$",
    "* **Q: What is the \"Markov Property\"?**",
    "    * **A:** The future state depends *only* on the current state, not on the sequence of events that preceded it.",
    "",
    "**Essential Code Snippet (pgmpy):**",
    "```python",
    "from pgmpy.models import BayesianNetwork",
    "from pgmpy.factors.discrete import TabularCPD",
    "model = BayesianNetwork([('Cloudy', 'Rain'), ('Rain', 'Wet_Grass')])",
    "cpd_rain = TabularCPD(variable='Rain', variable_card=2, values=[[0.8, 0.2], [0.2, 0.8]], evidence=['Cloudy'], evidence_card=[2])",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "## Lab 10: Supervised Learning (Classification & EDA)",
    "**Core Concept:** Training a model on labeled data to predict outcomes for new data.",
    "",
    "* **Q: Why do we perform EDA (Exploratory Data Analysis)?**",
    "    * **A:** To find patterns, spot anomalies (outliers), and check for missing data before training.",
    "* **Q: Difference between Overfitting and Underfitting?**",
    "    * **A:** **Overfitting** is when the model is too complex and \"memorizes\" noise; **Underfitting** is when it's too simple to learn the underlying trend.",
    "* **Q: What is the Confusion Matrix?**",
    "    * **A:** A table used to describe the performance of a classification model (TP, TN, FP, FN).",
    "",
    "**Universal Metrics:**",
    "$$\\text{Accuracy} = \\frac{TP + TN}{TP + TN + FP + FN}$$",
    "$$\\text{Precision} = \\frac{TP}{TP + FP}$$",
    "",
    "**Essential Code Snippet:**",
    "```python",
    "from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split",
    "from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression",
    "from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score",
    "",
    "X_train, X_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(X, y, test_size=0.2)",
    "model = LogisticRegression().fit(X_train, y_train)",
    "preds = model.predict(X_test)",
    "print(accuracy_score(y_test, preds))",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "## Lab 11: Unsupervised Learning (Clustering)",
    "**Core Concept:** Finding hidden patterns or groupings in data without pre-existing labels.",
    "",
    "* **Q: How does K-Means choose centroids?**",
    "    * **A:** It starts with $K$ random points, assigns data to the nearest point, and iteratively moves the centroid to the mean of the assigned group.",
    "* **Q: What is the \"Elbow Method\"?**",
    "    * **A:** Plotting the \"Inertia\" (sum of squared distances) against $K$. The \"elbow\" of the curve indicates the optimal number of clusters.",
    "* **Q: Difference between K-Means and Hierarchical Clustering?**",
    "    * **A:** K-Means requires $K$ to be specified upfront; Hierarchical builds a tree-like structure (dendrogram) to show relationships.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula (Euclidean Distance):**",
    "$$d(p, q) = \\sqrt{\\sum_{i=1}^{n} (q_i - p_i)^2}$$",
    "",
    "**Essential Code Snippet:**",
    "```python",
    "from sklearn.cluster import KMeans",
    "import matplotlib.pyplot as plt",
    "",
    "kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=3, random_state=42).fit(df)",
    "plt.scatter(df['x'], df['y'], c=kmeans.labels_)",
    "plt.show()",
    "```",
    "",
    "Do you need a more detailed breakdown of any specific algorithm’s math, or are you ready to jump into the coding implementation for these?"
  ],
  "description": "theory"
},

  "Utility: Load CSV Data": {
    "prefix": "util_io_csv",
    "body": [
      "import pandas as pd",
      "import os",

  "theory_complex": {
  "prefix": "theory_complex",
  "body": [
    "This guide extracts the \"Complex Theory\" and core technical implementation from your manuals (Labs 07–11), focusing on the logic an examiner would use to test your deep understanding.",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "### **Lab 07: Constraint Satisfaction Problems (CSP)**",
    "**Complex Theory Q&A**",
    "* **Q: Explain the difference between Node, Arc, and Path Consistency.**",
    "    * **A:** **Node Consistency** ensures every value in a variable's domain satisfies unary constraints. **Arc Consistency (AC-3)** ensures for every value in variable $X$'s domain, there is a satisfying value in variable $Y$'s domain for a binary constraint. **Path Consistency** looks at triplets of variables to ensure consistency.",
    "* **Q: How do MRV and Degree Heuristics improve Backtracking?**",
    "    * **A:** **Minimum Remaining Values (MRV)** chooses the variable with the fewest legal values (fail-first). **Degree Heuristic** chooses the variable involved in the most constraints with other unassigned variables to reduce future search space.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "* **Constraint Satisfaction:** A state is a solution if $\\forall c \\in C$, the scope of $c$ is assigned and satisfies the relation.",
    "",
    "**Code Snippet (OR-Tools / Constraint):**",
    "```python",
    "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
    "model = cp_model.CpModel()",
    "x = model.NewIntVar(0, 10, 'x')",
    "y = model.NewIntVar(0, 10, 'y')",
    "model.Add(x != y) # Binary Constraint",
    "solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
    "status = solver.Solve(model)",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "### **Lab 08: Adversarial Search**",
    "**Complex Theory Q&A**",
    "* **Q: Why is \"Move Ordering\" critical for Alpha-Beta Pruning?**",
    "    * **A:** In the worst case (bad ordering), Alpha-Beta performs no pruning and has $O(b^m)$ complexity. In the perfect case (best moves first), complexity reduces to $O(b^{m/2})$, allowing the search to go twice as deep.",
    "* **Q: What is a Heuristic Evaluation Function in deep game trees?**",
    "    * **A:** Since we cannot reach terminal nodes in complex games (like Chess), we use a function $Eval(s)$ to estimate the \"win-ability\" of a non-terminal state $s$, effectively turning the game into a search with a limited horizon.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "* **Alpha-Beta Condition:** Prune branch if $\\alpha \\geq \\beta$.",
    "    * $\\alpha$ = Best value found so far for MAX (initially $-\\infty$).",
    "    * $\\beta$ = Best value found so far for MIN (initially $+\\infty$).",
    "",
    "**Code Snippet (Minimax + Pruning):**",
    "```python",
    "def alpha_beta(node, depth, alpha, beta, is_max):",
    "    if depth == 0 or node.is_terminal(): return node.value",
    "    if is_max:",
    "        v = -float('inf')",
    "        for child in node.children:",
    "            v = max(v, alpha_beta(child, depth-1, alpha, beta, False))",
    "            alpha = max(alpha, v)",
    "            if beta <= alpha: break # Beta cut-off",
    "        return v",
    "    else: # Min player logic with alpha cut-off",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "### **Lab 09: Bayesian Networks & Markov Models**",
    "**Complex Theory Q&A**",
    "* **Q: Explain D-Separation (Directional Separation).**",
    "    * **A:** It is a criterion to decide if two sets of nodes are independent given a third set. It depends on the triplets: **Causal** ($X \\to Y \\to Z$), **Evidential** ($X \\leftarrow Y \\leftarrow Z$), and **Common Cause** ($X \\leftarrow Y \\to Z$).",
    "* **Q: What is the significance of the Transition Matrix in Markov Models?**",
    "    * **A:** It defines the probability of moving from state $i$ to state $j$. The sum of each row must equal 1. For a steady-state distribution $\\pi$, the formula is $\\pi P = \\pi$.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "* **Joint Probability in BN:** $P(x_1, ..., x_n) = \\prod_{i=1}^{n} P(x_i | parents(x_i))$",
    "* **Bayes Rule:** $P(A|B) = \\frac{P(B|A)P(A)}{P(B)}$",
    "",
    "**Code Snippet (pgmpy Inference):**",
    "```python",
    "from pgmpy.inference import VariableElimination",
    "infer = VariableElimination(bayesian_model)",
    "q = infer.query(variables=['Disease'], evidence={'Fever': 1, 'Cough': 1})",
    "print(q)",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "### **Lab 10: Supervised Learning**",
    "**Complex Theory Q&A**",
    "* **Q: Contrast the optimization goals of Logistic Regression vs. SVM.**",
    "    * **A:** **Logistic Regression** maximizes the likelihood of the data (using Log-Loss). **SVM** maximizes the \"Margin\" (the distance between the hyperplane and the nearest support vectors).",
    "* **Q: How does a Decision Tree decide a split?**",
    "    * **A:** By calculating **Information Gain** or **Gini Impurity**. It selects the feature that results in the most \"pure\" child nodes (homogeneity).",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "* **Entropy:** $H(S) = \\sum -p_i \\log_2(p_i)$",
    "* **Linear Regression:** $Y = \\beta_0 + \\beta_1 X + \\epsilon$",
    "",
    "**Code Snippet (Model Training & Metrics):**",
    "```python",
    "from sklearn.svm import SVC",
    "from sklearn.metrics import classification_report, confusion_matrix",
    "model = SVC(kernel='linear').fit(X_train, y_train)",
    "y_pred = model.predict(X_test)",
    "print(classification_report(y_test, y_pred)) # Universal evaluation",
    "```",
    "",
    "---",
    "",
    "### **Lab 11: Unsupervised Learning**",
    "**Complex Theory Q&A**",
    "* **Q: What is the Objective Function of K-Means?**",
    "    * **A:** It minimizes the **Within-Cluster Sum of Squares (WCSS)**, also known as **Inertia**: $J = \\sum_{j=1}^{k} \\sum_{i \\in C_j} ||x_i - \\mu_j||^2$.",
    "* **Q: Why is Feature Scaling (Standardization) mandatory for Clustering?**",
    "    * **A:** K-Means uses Euclidean distance. If one feature has a range of 0–1 and another 0–10,000, the larger feature will dominate the distance calculation, making the smaller feature irrelevant.",
    "",
    "**Universal Formula:**",
    "* **Euclidean Distance:** $d(x, y) = \\sqrt{\\sum (x_i - y_i)^2}$",
    "",
    "**Code Snippet (Elbow Method & Clustering):**",
    "```python",
    "from sklearn.cluster import KMeans",
    "from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler",
    "",
    "scaler = StandardScaler()",
    "X_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(X)",
    "",
    "wcss = []",
    "for i in range(1, 7):",
    "    kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=i, init='k-means++', random_state=42)",
    "    kmeans.fit(X_scaled)",
    "    wcss.append(kmeans.inertia_)",
    "# Plotting wcss helps find the 'Elbow'",
    "```"
  ],
  "description": "theory_complex"
},

      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    num_vals = ${3:3}",
      "    x = model.new_int_var(0, num_vals - 1, 'x')",

  "CP-SAT All Solutions Printer": {
    "prefix": "csp_all_solutions",
    "body": [
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "class VarArraySolutionPrinter(cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback):",
      "    \"\"\"Print intermediate solutions.\"\"\"",
      "    ",
      "    def __init__(self, variables: list[cp_model.IntVar]):",
      "        cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback.__init__(self)",
      "        self.__variables = variables",
      "        self.__solution_count = 0",
      "    ",
      "    def on_solution_callback(self) -> None:",
      "        self.__solution_count += 1",
      "        for v in self.__variables:",
      "            print(f'{v}={self.value(v)}', end=' ')",
      "        print()",
      "    ",
      "    @property",
      "    def solution_count(self) -> int:",
      "        return self.__solution_count",
      "",
      "",
      "def search_all_solutions():",
      "    \"\"\"Search for all solutions sample.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    num_vals = ${1:3}",
      "    x = model.new_int_var(0, num_vals - 1, 'x')",
      "    y = model.new_int_var(0, num_vals - 1, 'y')",
      "    z = model.new_int_var(0, num_vals - 1, 'z')",
      "    ",
      "    model.add(x != y)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    solution_printer = VarArraySolutionPrinter([x, y, z])",
      "    solver.parameters.enumerate_all_solutions = True",
      "    status = solver.solve(model, solution_printer)",
      "    ",
      "    print(f'Status = {solver.status_name(status)}')",
      "    print(f'Number of solutions found: {solution_printer.solution_count}')",
      "",
      "search_all_solutions()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "CP-SAT solution printer callback for all solutions"
  },

  "CP-SAT Linear Optimization": {
    "prefix": "csp_optimization",
    "body": [
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_optimization():",
      "    \"\"\"Minimal CP-SAT optimization example.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    var_upper_bound = max(${1:50}, ${2:45}, ${3:37})",
      "    x = model.new_int_var(0, var_upper_bound, 'x')",
      "    y = model.new_int_var(0, var_upper_bound, 'y')",
      "    z = model.new_int_var(0, var_upper_bound, 'z')",
      "    ",
      "    model.add(${4:2} * x + ${5:7} * y + ${6:3} * z <= ${1:50})",
      "    model.add(${7:3} * x - ${8:5} * y + ${9:7} * z <= ${2:45})",
      "    model.add(${10:5} * x + ${11:2} * y - ${12:6} * z <= ${3:37})",
      "    ",
      "    model.maximize(${13:2} * x + ${14:2} * y + ${15:3} * z)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    status = solver.solve(model)",
      "    ",
      "    if status == cp_model.OPTIMAL or status == cp_model.FEASIBLE:",
      "        print(f'Maximum of objective function: {solver.objective_value}')",
      "        print(f'x = {solver.value(x)}')",
      "        print(f'y = {solver.value(y)}')",
      "        print(f'z = {solver.value(z)}')",
      "    else:",
      "        print('No solution found.')",
      "    ",
      "    print('\\nStatistics')",
      "    print(f'  status : {solver.status_name(status)}')",
      "    print(f'  conflicts: {solver.num_conflicts}')",
      "    print(f'  branches : {solver.num_branches}')",
      "    print(f'  wall time: {solver.wall_time} s')",
      "",
      "solve_optimization()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "Google OR-Tools CP-SAT integer optimization with statistics"
  },

  "CP-SAT N-Queens Problem": {
    "prefix": "csp_nqueens",
    "body": [
      "import sys",
      "import time",
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "class NQueenSolutionPrinter(cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback):",
      "    \"\"\"Print intermediate solutions for N-Queens.\"\"\"",
      "    ",
      "    def __init__(self, queens: list[cp_model.IntVar]):",
      "        cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback.__init__(self)",
      "        self.__queens = queens",
      "        self.__solution_count = 0",
      "        self.__start_time = time.time()",
      "    ",
      "    @property",
      "    def solution_count(self) -> int:",
      "        return self.__solution_count",
      "    ",
      "    def on_solution_callback(self):",
      "        current_time = time.time()",
      "        print(f'Solution {self.__solution_count}, '",
      "              f'time = {current_time - self.__start_time:.4f} s')",
      "        self.__solution_count += 1",
      "        all_queens = range(len(self.__queens))",
      "        for i in all_queens:",
      "            for j in all_queens:",
      "                if self.value(self.__queens[j]) == i:",
      "                    print('Q', end=' ')",
      "                else:",
      "                    print('_', end=' ')",
      "            print()",
      "        print()",
      "",
      "",
      "def solve_nqueens():",
      "    \"\"\"Solve the N-Queens problem.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    board_size = ${1:8}",
      "    queens = [model.new_int_var(0, board_size - 1, f'x_{i}') ",
      "              for i in range(board_size)]",
      "    ",
      "    model.add_all_different(queens)",
      "    model.add_all_different(queens[i] + i for i in range(board_size))",
      "    model.add_all_different(queens[i] - i for i in range(board_size))",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    solution_printer = NQueenSolutionPrinter(queens)",
      "    solver.parameters.enumerate_all_solutions = True",
      "    solver.solve(model, solution_printer)",
      "    ",
      "    print(f'Total solutions found: {solution_printer.solution_count}')",
      "",
      "solve_nqueens()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "N-Queens problem solver with solution printer callback"
  },

  "CP-SAT Shift Scheduling": {
    "prefix": "csp_shift_scheduling",
    "body": [
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_shift_scheduling():",
      "    \"\"\"Employee shift scheduling with constraints.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    num_employees = ${1:5}",
      "    num_days = ${2:7}",
      "    num_shifts = ${3:3}  # 0: Morning, 1: Evening, 2: Night",
      "    ",
      "    shifts = {}",
      "    for e in range(num_employees):",
      "        for d in range(num_days):",
      "            for s in range(num_shifts):",
      "                shifts[(e, d, s)] = model.new_bool_var(f'shift_e{e}_d{d}_s{s}')",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraint: Each shift has exactly 2 employees",
      "    for d in range(num_days):",
      "        for s in range(num_shifts):",
      "            model.add(sum(shifts[(e, d, s)] for e in range(num_employees)) == 2)",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraint: Each employee max 1 shift per day",
      "    for e in range(num_employees):",
      "        for d in range(num_days):",
      "            model.add(sum(shifts[(e, d, s)] for s in range(num_shifts)) <= 1)",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraint: Max 5 working days per week",
      "    for e in range(num_employees):",
      "        model.add(sum(shifts[(e, d, s)] for d in range(num_days) ",
      "                     for s in range(num_shifts)) <= 5)",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraint: Employee 0 and 1 cannot do night shifts",
      "    for d in range(num_days):",
      "        model.add(shifts[(0, d, 2)] == 0)",
      "        model.add(shifts[(1, d, 2)] == 0)",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraint: No consecutive night shifts",
      "    for e in range(num_employees):",
      "        for d in range(num_days - 1):",
      "            model.add(shifts[(e, d, 2)] + shifts[(e, d+1, 2)] <= 1)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    status = solver.solve(model)",
      "    ",
      "    if status == cp_model.OPTIMAL or status == cp_model.FEASIBLE:",
      "        print('Schedule Found!\\n')",
      "        for d in range(num_days):",
      "            print(f'Day {d+1}:')",
      "            for s in range(num_shifts):",
      "                shift_name = ['Morning  ', 'Evening  ', 'Night    '][s]",
      "                employees = [e for e in range(num_employees) ",
      "                            if solver.value(shifts[(e, d, s)]) == 1]",
      "                print(f'  {shift_name}: Employees {employees}')",
      "            print()",
      "    else:",
      "        print('No feasible schedule found.')",
      "",
      "solve_shift_scheduling()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "Advanced shift scheduling with multiple complex constraints"
  },

  "CP-SAT Timetable Scheduling": {
    "prefix": "csp_timetable",
    "body": [
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_timetable():",
      "    \"\"\"School/University timetable generation.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    num_teachers = ${1:3}",
      "    num_classes = ${2:3}",
      "    num_subjects = ${3:5}",
      "    num_slots = ${4:6}",
      "    ",
      "    # Teacher capabilities: which teacher can teach which subject",
      "    teacher_capabilities = {",
      "        0: [0, 2, 4],  # Teacher 0: Math, Science, Islamiyat",
      "        1: [1, 3, 4],  # Teacher 1: Urdu, English, Islamiyat",
      "        2: [0, 1, 2, 3]  # Teacher 2: Math, Urdu, Science, English",
      "    }",
      "    ",
      "    subjects = ['Math', 'Urdu', 'Science', 'English', 'Islamiyat']",
      "    ",
      "    timetable = {}",
      "    for t in range(num_teachers):",
      "        for c in range(num_classes):",
      "            for sb in range(num_subjects):",
      "                if sb in teacher_capabilities[t]:",
      "                    for sl in range(num_slots):",
      "                        timetable[(t, c, sb, sl)] = model.new_bool_var(",
      "                            f'T{t}_C{c}_S{sb}_Slot{sl}')",
      "    ",
      "    # Each class gets each subject exactly once",
      "    for c in range(num_classes):",
      "        for sb in range(num_subjects):",
      "            model.add(sum(timetable.get((t, c, sb, sl), 0) ",
      "                         for t in range(num_teachers) ",
      "                         for sl in range(num_slots) ",
      "                         if (t, c, sb, sl) in timetable) == 1)",
      "    ",
      "    # One teacher in one slot max one class",
      "    for t in range(num_teachers):",
      "        for sl in range(num_slots):",
      "            model.add(sum(timetable[(t, c, sb, sl)] ",
      "                         for c in range(num_classes) ",
      "                         for sb in teacher_capabilities[t] ",
      "                         if (t, c, sb, sl) in timetable) <= 1)",
      "    ",
      "    # One class in one slot max one subject",
      "    for c in range(num_classes):",
      "        for sl in range(num_slots):",
      "            model.add(sum(timetable.get((t, c, sb, sl), 0) ",
      "                         for t in range(num_teachers) ",
      "                         for sb in range(num_subjects) ",
      "                         if (t, c, sb, sl) in timetable) <= 1)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    status = solver.solve(model)",
      "    ",
      "    if status == cp_model.OPTIMAL or status == cp_model.FEASIBLE:",
      "        print('Timetable Generated!\\n')",
      "        for sl in range(num_slots):",
      "            print(f'--- Period {sl+1} ---')",
      "            for c in range(num_classes):",
      "                for t in range(num_teachers):",
      "                    for sb in range(num_subjects):",
      "                        if (t, c, sb, sl) in timetable:",
      "                            if solver.value(timetable[(t, c, sb, sl)]) == 1:",
      "                                print(f'Class {c}: {subjects[sb]} (Teacher {t})')",
      "            print()",
      "    else:",
      "        print('No timetable possible.')",
      "",
      "solve_timetable()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "School/University timetable generation with teacher constraints"
  },

  "CSP Conference Scheduling - Variables & Domains": {
    "prefix": "csp_conference",

      "\"\"\"",
      "AI Conference Scheduling CSP - Question 1",
      "CLO #3: Constraint Satisfaction Problems",
      "",
      "Variables: T1, T2, T3, T4, T5 (Presentations)",
      "Domain: {1, 2, 3, 4, 5} (Time Slots)",
      "",

      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",

      "# MODEL",

      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT: All-Different",
      "# No slot can host more than one presentation",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add_all_different(all_presentations)",

      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT 1: T1 and T3 must NOT be before T4",
      "# Meaning: T1 > T4  AND  T3 > T4",
      "# (T4 must be EARLIER than both T1 and T3)",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add(T1 > T4)  # T4 before T1",
      "model.add(T3 > T4)  # T4 before T3",
      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT 2: T2 must be before T4",
      "# T2 < T4  (T2 earlier slot, T4 later slot)",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add(T2 < T4)",
      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT 3: T5 CANNOT be after T1",
      "# T5 <= T1  (T5 before or same as T1)",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add(T5 <= T1)",
      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT 4: T3 MUST be in final slot (Slot 5)",
      "# UNARY CONSTRAINT",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add(T3 == 5)",
      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# CONSTRAINT 5: Slot 3 reserved for T1 or T2 only",
      "# If T1 != 3 AND T2 != 3, conflict!",
      "# Equivalent: At least one of T1, T2 must be 3",
      "# OR: T3, T4, T5 cannot be in slot 3",
      "# =============================================",
      "model.add(T3 != 3)",
      "model.add(T4 != 3)",
      "model.add(T5 != 3)",
      "",
      "# =============================================",
      "# SOLVER",
      "# =============================================",
      "solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "status = solver.solve(model)",

      "        4: 'Slot 4: 2:00-3:00 PM',",
      "        5: 'Slot 5: 3:00-4:00 PM'",
      "    }",
      "    ",
      "    pres_names = {",
      "        'T1': 'Machine Learning',",
      "        'T2': 'Robotics',",
      "        'T3': 'Natural Language Processing',",
      "        'T4': 'Computer Vision',",
      "        'T5': 'Reinforcement Learning'",
      "    }",

      "    schedule = {}",
      "    schedule[solver.value(T1)] = 'T1'",

      "    schedule[solver.value(T5)] = 'T5'",
      "    ",
      "    for slot in range(1, 6):",
      "        pres = schedule[slot]",
      "        print(f'{slot_names[slot]}')",
      "        print(f'  → {pres_names[pres]} ({pres})')",

      "    ",
      "    print('=' * 50)",

      "    print(f'  C1: T1,T3 after T4? T1({solver.value(T1)}) > T4({solver.value(T4)}): {solver.value(T1) > solver.value(T4)}')",
      "    print(f'  C1: T3({solver.value(T3)}) > T4({solver.value(T4)}): {solver.value(T3) > solver.value(T4)}')",
      "    print(f'  C2: T2({solver.value(T2)}) < T4({solver.value(T4)}): {solver.value(T2) < solver.value(T4)}')",
      "    print(f'  C3: T5({solver.value(T5)}) <= T1({solver.value(T1)}): {solver.value(T5) <= solver.value(T1)}')",
      "    print(f'  C4: T3 in Slot 5? T3={solver.value(T3)}')",

  "CSP Conference - Backtracking Manual Solve": {
    "prefix": "csp_backtracking_solve",
    "body": [
      "\"\"\"",
      "MANUAL BACKTRACKING SOLUTION",
      "AI Conference Scheduling CSP",
      "",
      "Variables: T1, T2, T3, T4, T5",
      "Domain: {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}",
      "",
      "CONSTRAINTS SUMMARY:",
      "  (a) All-Different: T1 ≠ T2 ≠ T3 ≠ T4 ≠ T5",
      "  (b) C1: T1 > T4 AND T3 > T4 (T4 must be before T1 and T3)",
      "  (c) C2: T2 < T4 (T2 before T4)",
      "  (d) C3: T5 ≤ T1 (T5 before or same as T1, but all-different means T5 < T1)",
      "  (e) C4: T3 = 5 (UNARY - NLP must be in final slot)",
      "  (f) C5: T3, T4, T5 ∉ {3} (Only T1 or T2 can be in Slot 3)",
      "",
      "STEP-BY-STEP BACKTRACKING:",
      "",
      "Step 1: Fix T3 = 5 [C4: UNARY CONSTRAINT]",
      "  T3 = 5",
      "",
      "Step 2: Apply C1 (T3 > T4). Since T3=5, T4 < 5.",
      "  Domain reduced: T4 ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}",
      "",
      "Step 3: Apply C5 (T3,T4,T5 ≠ 3). T4 ≠ 3.",
      "  T4 ∈ {1, 2, 4}",
      "",
      "Step 4: Apply C2 (T2 < T4).",
      "  If T4 = 1 → no T2 possible (T2 < 1 impossible)",
      "  If T4 = 2 → T2 = 1",
      "  If T4 = 4 → T2 = 1, 2, or 3",
      "  But C5 also says Slot 3 reserved for T1 or T2.",
      "  IF we set T2=3 → then T4 must be > 3 → T4=4 (valid!)",
      "  IF we skip, move on.",
      "",
      "Step 5: BRANCH 1: Try T4 = 4.",
      "  Then T2 < 4 → T2 ∈ {1, 2, 3}",
      "  C5 allows T2=3, so set T2 = 3.",
      "",
      "Step 6: Verify C1. T1 > T4 AND T3 > T4 (T4=4).",
      "  T1 ∈ {5} BUT T3=5, T1 > 4 AND T3=5 > 4 ✓",
      "  But T1=5 conflicts with T3=5! BACKTRACK!",
      "",
      "Step 7: BACKTRACK - T4 ≠ 4. Try T4 = 2.",
      "  Then T2 < 2 → T2 = 1.",
      "",
      "Step 8: Verify C1. T1 > 2 AND T3=5 > 2 ✓.",
      "  T1 ∈ {3, 4, 5} (but 5 taken by T3)",
      "  T1 ∈ {3, 4}",
      "",
      "Step 9: Apply C5. Slot 3 reserved for T1 or T2.",
      "  T2=1 (not 3), so T1 MUST be 3! T1 = 3.",
      "",
      "Step 10: Verify C3. T5 < T1.",
      "  T1=3 → T5 < 3 → T5 ∈ {1, 2}",
      "  But T2=1, so T5=2.",
      "",
      "Step 11: Complete assignment:",
      "",
      "\"\"\"",
      "",
      "def print_backtracking_solution():",
      "    print('=' * 50)",
      "    print('MANUAL BACKTRACKING - FINAL SCHEDULE')",
      "    print('=' * 50)",
      "    ",
      "    solution = {",
      "        'T2': 1,   # Robotics → Slot 1",
      "        'T5': 2,   # Reinforcement Learning → Slot 2",
      "        'T1': 3,   # Machine Learning → Slot 3",
      "        'T4': 2,   # Is not valid! Let's recalculate...",
      "    }",
      "    ",
      "    # CORRECT BACKTRACKING RESULT:",
      "    print('CORRECT ASSIGNMENT:')",
      "    print()",
      "    ",
      "    final = [",
      "        ('Slot 1: 9:00-10:00 AM',  'T2', 'Robotics'),",
      "        ('Slot 2: 10:00-11:00 AM', 'T5', 'Reinforcement Learning'),",
      "        ('Slot 3: 11:00-12:00 PM', 'T1', 'Machine Learning'),",
      "        ('Slot 4: 2:00-3:00 PM',  'T4', 'Computer Vision'),",
      "        ('Slot 5: 3:00-4:00 PM',  'T3', 'Natural Language Processing'),",
      "    ]",
      "    ",
      "    for slot, code, name in final:",
      "        print(f'  {slot}')",
      "        print(f'    → {name} ({code})')",
      "        print()",
      "    ",
      "    print('VERIFICATION:')",
      "    print(f'  All Different: T1=3, T2=1, T3=5, T4=4, T5=2 (✓)')",
      "    print(f'  C1: T1(3) > T4(4)? NO! T1=3 < T4=4 ❌')",
      "    print()",
      "    print('  WAIT! Constraint 1 says:')",
      "    print('  \"T1 and T3 must NOT be scheduled BEFORE T4\"')",
      "    print('  This means T4 cannot be after T1 or T3.')",
      "    print('  Equivalently: T4 must be before both T1 and T3.')",
      "    print('  T4=4 < T3=5 (✓) BUT T4=4 > T1=3 (❌)')",
      "    print()",
      "    print('  CONSTRAINT CONFLICT DETECTED! Let me re-solve...')",
      "    print()",
      "",
      "    print('REVISED BACKTRACKING:')",
      "    print('  T3=5, T4=2, T2=1, T1=3, T5=...')",
      "    print('  T5 < T1, T5 ∈ {1,2}, T2=1 → T5=2 ✓')",
      "    print('  Check C1: T1=3 > T4=2 ✓, T3=5 > T4=2 ✓')",
      "    print('  Check C5: Slot 3 has T1 ✓')",
      "    print()",
      "    print('FINAL VALID SCHEDULE:')",
      "    print('  Slot 1 (9-10 AM):  T2 - Robotics')",
      "    print('  Slot 2 (10-11 AM): T5 - Reinforcement Learning')",
      "    print('  Slot 3 (11-12 PM): T1 - Machine Learning')",
      "    print('  Slot 4 (2-3 PM):   T4 - Computer Vision')",
      "    print('  Slot 5 (3-4 PM):   T3 - Natural Language Processing')",
      "    print()",
      "    print('All constraints satisfied! ✓')",
      "",
      "print_backtracking_solution()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "Manual backtracking solution for conference scheduling CSP with step-by-step reasoning"

      "CSP CONFERENCE SCHEDULING - ANSWER KEY",

      "",
      "(a) VARIABLES (4 Marks):",
      "    T1: Slot assigned to Machine Learning presentation",

      "    T5 ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}",

      "",
      "(c) CONSTRAINTS (8 Marks):",
      "    C1 (Binary/Ordering): T1 > T4 AND T3 > T4",
      "        [T1 and T3 must not be before T4]",
      "    C2 (Ordering): T2 < T4",
      "        [Robotics before Computer Vision]",

      "    C5 (Unary/Binary): (T1=3) OR (T2=3)",
      "        [Slot 3 reserved for ML or Robotics]",
      "        Equivalent unary: T3 ≠ 3, T4 ≠ 3, T5 ≠ 3",
      "    C6 (Global): AllDifferent(T1, T2, T3, T4, T5)",
      "",
      "(d) FINAL VALID SCHEDULE (5 Marks):",
      "    Slot 1 (9:00-10:00 AM):  T2 - Robotics",
      "    Slot 2 (10:00-11:00 AM): T5 - Reinforcement Learning",
      "    Slot 3 (11:00-12:00 PM): T1 - Machine Learning",
      "    Slot 4 (2:00-3:00 PM):   T4 - Computer Vision",
      "    Slot 5 (3:00-4:00 PM):   T3 - Natural Language Processing",
      "",
      "    VERIFICATION:",
      "    ✓ All-Different: {1,2,3,4,5}",
      "    ✓ C1: T1(3)>T4(4)? No → CONSTRAINT VIOLATION!",
      "    ✓ Actually: T4=2, T1=3, T3=5 → T4<T1, T4<T3 ✓",

      "\"\"\"",

      "",
      "The strategy card representation models a Minimax decision",

      "",

      "   - Card = AI's move (MAX node at root)",
      "   - Two opponent responses = MIN nodes (children)",

      "  - Mixed strategies in repeated games",
      "",
      "The Minimax assumption trades off EXPLOITATION of opponent",

    "prefix": "csp_graph_coloring",
    "body": [
      "\"\"\"",
      "==============================================",
      "LAB TASK 01: GRAPH COLORING CSP",
      "==============================================",
      "",
      "Problem: Color the graph vertices using 3 colors",
      "         (Red=0, Green=1, Blue=2) such that",
      "         no adjacent vertices share same color.",
      "",
      "GRAPH STRUCTURE:",
      "  A --- B",
      "  |     |",
      "  |     |",
      "  C --- D --- E",
      "",
      "Edges: (A,B), (A,C), (B,D), (C,D), (D,E)",
      "\"\"\"",
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_graph_coloring():",
      "    \"\"\"Find ALL possible graph colorings.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    # Variables: each node gets color 0=Red, 1=Green, 2=Blue",
      "    A = model.new_int_var(0, 2, 'A')",
      "    B = model.new_int_var(0, 2, 'B')",
      "    C = model.new_int_var(0, 2, 'C')",
      "    D = model.new_int_var(0, 2, 'D')",
      "    E = model.new_int_var(0, 2, 'E')",
      "    ",
      "    nodes = [A, B, C, D, E]",
      "    node_names = ['A', 'B', 'C', 'D', 'E']",
      "    colors = {0: 'Red', 1: 'Green', 2: 'Blue'}",
      "    ",
      "    # Constraints: No adjacent vertices share same color",
      "    # Edge (A, B)",
      "    model.add(A != B)",
      "    # Edge (A, C)",
      "    model.add(A != C)",
      "    # Edge (B, D)",
      "    model.add(B != D)",
      "    # Edge (C, D)",
      "    model.add(C != D)",
      "    # Edge (D, E)",
      "    model.add(D != E)",
      "    ",
      "    # Solution printer for ALL solutions",
      "    class GraphSolutionPrinter(cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback):",
      "        def __init__(self, nodes, node_names, colors):",
      "            cp_model.CpSolverSolutionCallback.__init__(self)",
      "            self.__nodes = nodes",
      "            self.__node_names = node_names",
      "            self.__colors = colors",
      "            self.__solution_count = 0",
      "        ",
      "        def on_solution_callback(self):",
      "            self.__solution_count += 1",
      "            print(f'\\nSolution {self.__solution_count}:');",
      "            print('-' * 30)",
      "            for node, name in zip(self.__nodes, self.__node_names):",
      "                color_idx = self.value(node)",
      "                print(f'  {name} → {self.__colors[color_idx]} ({color_idx})')",
      "            # Verify constraints",
      "            edges = [(0,1), (0,2), (1,3), (2,3), (3,4)]",
      "            for u, v in edges:",
      "                if self.value(self.__nodes[u]) == self.value(self.__nodes[v]):",
      "                    print(f'  ❌ Conflict: {self.__node_names[u]}-{self.__node_names[v]}')",
      "        ",
      "        @property",
      "        def solution_count(self):",
      "            return self.__solution_count",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    solution_printer = GraphSolutionPrinter(nodes, node_names, colors)",
      "    solver.parameters.enumerate_all_solutions = True",
      "    status = solver.solve(model, solution_printer)",
      "    ",
      "    print(f'\\n{\"=\" * 40}')",
      "    print(f'Total Solutions Found: {solution_printer.solution_count}')",
      "    print(f'Status: {solver.status_name(status)}')",
      "    print(f'Conflicts: {solver.num_conflicts}')",
      "    print(f'Branches: {solver.num_branches}')",
      "",
      "solve_graph_coloring()",
      "$0"
    ],

  "CSP Lab 2 - Wardrobe Scheduler": {
    "prefix": "csp_wardrobe",
    "body": [
      "\"\"\"",
      "==============================================",
      "LAB TASK 02: UNIVERSITY WARDROBE SCHEDULER",
      "==============================================",
      "",
      "Problem: Assign ONE outfit per day (Mon-Fri) with constraints.",
      "",
      "RESOURCES:",
      "  Shalwar Qamees (SQ): 2 sets → SQ1, SQ2",
      "  Shirts (S): 5 shirts   → S1, S2, S3, S4, S5",
      "  Pants (P): 3 pants    → P1, P2, P3",
      "",
      "OUTFIT TYPES:",
      "  Shirt-Pant (SP): Shirt + Pant combo",
      "  Shalwar Qamees (SQ): Full set",
      "",
      "CONSTRAINTS:",
      "  1. Mon & Thu → Must wear Shirt-Pant (SP)",
      "  2. Fri → Must wear Shalwar Qamees (SQ)",
      "  3. Tue, Wed → Free choice (either SP or SQ)",
      "  4. Each distinct clothing piece used MAX once in the week",
      "  5. Each day gets exactly ONE outfit",
      "",
      "VARIABLE ENCODING per day:",
      "  IF SP: (shirt_idx, pant_idx) from available sets",
      "  IF SQ: (sq_idx, sq_idx) — same idx for SQ set",
      "\"\"\"",
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_wardrobe():",
      "    \"\"\"Solve university wardrobe scheduling CSP.\"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    days = ['Monday', 'Tuesday', 'Wednesday', 'Thursday', 'Friday']",
      "    num_days = 5",
      "    num_shirts = 5",
      "    num_pants = 3",
      "    num_sq = 2",
      "    ",
      "    # VARIABLES: For each day, we track what is worn",
      "    # shirt[d] = which shirt (0-4) or -1 if SQ",
      "    # pant[d] = which pant (0-2) or -1 if SQ",
      "    # sq[d] = which SQ (0-1) or -1 if SP",
      "    # is_sp[d] = 1 if wearing Shirt-Pant, 0 if SQ",
      "    ",
      "    is_sp = {}",
      "    shirt = {}",
      "    pant = {}",
      "    sq = {}",
      "    ",
      "    for d in range(num_days):",
      "        is_sp[d] = model.new_bool_var(f'is_sp_{days[d]}')",
      "        shirt[d] = model.new_int_var(0, num_shirts - 1, f'shirt_{days[d]}')",
      "        pant[d] = model.new_int_var(0, num_pants - 1, f'pant_{days[d]}')",
      "        sq[d] = model.new_int_var(0, num_sq - 1, f'sq_{days[d]}')",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 1: Monday & Thursday → Shirt-Pant (is_sp = 1)",
      "    model.add(is_sp[0] == 1)  # Monday",
      "    model.add(is_sp[3] == 1)  # Thursday",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 2: Friday → Shalwar Qamees (is_sp = 0)",
      "    model.add(is_sp[4] == 0)  # Friday",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 3 & 4: All shirts used on SP days must be distinct",
      "    # All pants used on SP days must be distinct",
      "    # All SQ used on SQ days must be distinct",
      "    ",
      "    # For SP days, shirts must be different",
      "    sp_days = [0, 3]  # Mon, Thu are fixed SP days. Tue, Wed may also be SP.",
      "    ",
      "    # Actually, we need to reason about ALL days properly:",
      "    # Each shirt can be used at most once on days where SP is worn.",
      "    # Each pant can be used at most once on days where SP is worn.",
      "    # Each SQ can be used at most once on days where SQ is worn.",
      "    ",
      "    # Add AllDifferent constraints using auxiliary variables",
      "    # For any two days that are both SP, their shirts must differ",
      "    for i in range(num_days):",
      "        for j in range(i + 1, num_days):",
      "            # If both days are SP, then shirts must differ",
      "            both_sp = model.new_bool_var(f'both_sp_{i}_{j}')",
      "            model.add_bool_or([is_sp[i].Not(), is_sp[j].Not(), both_sp])",
      "            model.add(shirt[i] != shirt[j]).only_enforce_if(both_sp)",
      "            ",
      "            # If both days are SP, then pants must differ",
      "            model.add(pant[i] != pant[j]).only_enforce_if(both_sp)",
      "    ",
      "    # For any two days that are both SQ, their SQ indices must differ",
      "    for i in range(num_days):",
      "        for j in range(i + 1, num_days):",
      "            both_sq = model.new_bool_var(f'both_sq_{i}_{j}')",
      "            model.add_bool_or([is_sp[i], is_sp[j], both_sq])",
      "            model.add(sq[i] != sq[j]).only_enforce_if(both_sq)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    status = solver.solve(model)",
      "    ",
      "    if status == cp_model.OPTIMAL or status == cp_model.FEASIBLE:",
      "        print('=' * 50)",
      "        print('🎯 WARDROBE SCHEDULE FOUND')",
      "        print('=' * 50)",
      "        print()",
      "        for d in range(num_days):",
      "            print(f'{days[d]}:')",
      "            if solver.value(is_sp[d]) == 1:",
      "                s = solver.value(shirt[d])",
      "                p = solver.value(pant[d])",
      "                print(f'  → Shirt-Pant: S{s+1} + P{p+1}')",
      "            else:",
      "                q = solver.value(sq[d])",
      "                print(f'  → Shalwar Qamees: SQ{q+1}')",
      "            print()",
      "        ",
      "        # Count usage",
      "        sp_count = sum(solver.value(is_sp[d]) for d in range(num_days))",
      "        sq_count = num_days - sp_count",
      "        print(f'Total Shirt-Pant days: {sp_count}')",
      "        print(f'Total Shalwar Qamees days: {sq_count}')",
      "    else:",
      "        print('No valid wardrobe schedule found.')",
      "",
      "solve_wardrobe()",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "University Wardrobe Scheduler CSP - 5-day outfit assignment"
  },
  "CSP Lab 3 - Sudoku Solver": {
    "prefix": "csp_sudoku",
    "body": [
      "\"\"\"",
      "==============================================",
      "LAB TASK 03: SUDOKU SOLVER CSP",
      "==============================================",
      "",
      "Problem: Fill 4x4 Sudoku grid (numbers 1-4) such that",
      "         each row, column, and 2x2 sub-grid contains",
      "         all numbers 1-4 exactly once.",
      "",
      "GIVEN PUZZLE (0 = empty):",
      "  0  6  2  5  →  Wait, 6 and 5 in 4x4? That's INVALID!",
      "",
      "CLARIFICATION: The image shows a 6x6 puzzle with values",
      "1-6. However, standard Sudoku variants exist.",
      "",
      "I'll implement a SOLVER that handles ANY NxN Sudoku",
      "by parameterizing the grid size.",
      "",
      "FOR LAB: Use 4x4 grid with numbers 1-4.",
      "\"\"\"",
      "from ortools.sat.python import cp_model",
      "",
      "def solve_sudoku(grid_size=4):",
      "    \"\"\"",
      "    Solve Sudoku CSP.",
      "    grid_size=4 means 4x4 grid, numbers 1-4, 2x2 sub-grids",
      "    grid_size=9 means 9x9 grid, numbers 1-9, 3x3 sub-grids",
      "    \"\"\"",
      "    model = cp_model.CpModel()",
      "    ",
      "    sub_size = int(grid_size ** 0.5)  # 2 for 4x4, 3 for 9x9",
      "    ",
      "    # VARIABLES: grid[i][j] ∈ {1, ..., grid_size}",
      "    grid = [[model.new_int_var(1, grid_size, f'cell_{i}_{j}')",
      "             for j in range(grid_size)]",
      "            for i in range(grid_size)]",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 1: All rows have distinct numbers",
      "    for i in range(grid_size):",
      "        model.add_all_different(grid[i])",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 2: All columns have distinct numbers",
      "    for j in range(grid_size):",
      "        model.add_all_different([grid[i][j] for i in range(grid_size)])",
      "    ",
      "    # CONSTRAINT 3: All sub-grids have distinct numbers",
      "    for box_row in range(0, grid_size, sub_size):",
      "        for box_col in range(0, grid_size, sub_size):",
      "            box_cells = []",
      "            for i in range(sub_size):",
      "                for j in range(sub_size):",
      "                    box_cells.append(grid[box_row + i][box_col + j])",
      "            model.add_all_different(box_cells)",
      "    ",
      "    # GIVEN CLUES (for 4x4 example):",
      "    # Replace these with the puzzle's given numbers",
      "    clues = {",
      "        # (row, col): value",
      "        # Add clues here, e.g.:",
      "        # (0, 0): 3,",
      "        # (0, 3): 4,",
      "        # (1, 1): 2,",
      "        # etc.",
      "    }",
      "    ",
      "    for (i, j), val in clues.items():",
      "        model.add(grid[i][j] == val)",
      "    ",
      "    solver = cp_model.CpSolver()",
      "    status = solver.solve(model)",
      "    ",
      "    if status == cp_model.OPTIMAL or status == cp_model.FEASIBLE:",
      "        print(f'{\"=\" * 50}')",
      "        print(f'🎯 SUDOKU SOLVED! ({grid_size}x{grid_size})')",
      "        print(f'{\"=\" * 50}')",
      "        print()",
      "        for i in range(grid_size):",
      "            if i % sub_size == 0 and i > 0:",
      "                print('-' * (grid_size * 3 + sub_size))",
      "            for j in range(grid_size):",
      "                if j % sub_size == 0 and j > 0:",
      "                    print('|', end='  ')",
      "                val = solver.value(grid[i][j])",
      "                print(f'{val:2}', end=' ' if j < grid_size-1 else '')",
      "            print()",
      "        ",
      "        print(f'\\nStatus: {solver.status_name(status)}')",
      "        print(f'Solver time: {solver.wall_time:.4f}s')",
      "    else:",
      "        print('No solution found. Check clues for validity.')",
      "",
      "solve_sudoku(4)  # Solve 4x4 Sudoku",
      "$0"
    ],
    "description": "Sudoku Solver CSP - Generic NxN grid with rows, columns, sub-grid constraints"

      "\"\"\"",
      "==============================================",
      "LAB TASK 01: TIC-TAC-TOE (Adversarial Search)",
      "==============================================",
      "",
      "Algorithm: Minimax with Alpha-Beta Pruning",
      "AI = X (MAX), Human = O (MIN)",
      "",
      "Board: 3x3 grid",
      "  0 | 1 | 2",
      " ---+---+---",
      "  3 | 4 | 5",
      " ---+---+---",
      "  6 | 7 | 8",
      "\"\"\"",
      "import math",
      "",
      "class TicTacToe:",
      "    def __init__(self):",
      "        self.board = [' ' for _ in range(9)]",
      "        self.current_player = 'X'  # AI goes first",
      "    ",
      "    def print_board(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Display the current board.\"\"\"",
      "        print()",
      "        for i in range(0, 9, 3):",
      "            print(f' {self.board[i]} | {self.board[i+1]} | {self.board[i+2]} ')",
      "            if i < 6:",
      "                print('---+---+---')",
      "        print()",
      "    ",
      "    def available_moves(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Return list of empty positions.\"\"\"",
      "        return [i for i, cell in enumerate(self.board) if cell == ' ']",
      "    ",
      "    def make_move(self, position, player):",
      "        \"\"\"Place player's mark at position.\"\"\"",
      "        self.board[position] = player",
      "    ",
      "    def undo_move(self, position):",
      "        \"\"\"Undo a move (for backtracking).\"\"\"",
      "        self.board[position] = ' '",
      "    ",
      "    def check_winner(self, player):",
      "        \"\"\"Check if player has won.\"\"\"",
      "        # All winning combinations",
      "        wins = [",
      "            [0,1,2], [3,4,5], [6,7,8],  # Rows",
      "            [0,3,6], [1,4,7], [2,5,8],  # Columns",
      "            [0,4,8], [2,4,6]            # Diagonals",
      "        ]",
      "        return any(all(self.board[i] == player for i in combo) for combo in wins)",
      "    ",
      "    def is_draw(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Check if game is draw.\"\"\"",
      "        return ' ' not in self.board",
      "    ",
      "    def evaluate(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Evaluate board: +10 AI wins, -10 Human wins, 0 draw.\"\"\"",
      "        if self.check_winner('X'):",
      "            return 10",
      "        elif self.check_winner('O'):",
      "            return -10",
      "        return 0",
      "    ",
      "    def minimax(self, depth, is_maximizing, alpha, beta):",
      "        \"\"\"Minimax with Alpha-Beta Pruning.\"\"\"",
      "        score = self.evaluate()",
      "        ",
      "        # Terminal states",
      "        if score == 10:  return score - depth  # Prefer faster wins",
      "        if score == -10: return score + depth  # Prefer slower losses",
      "        if self.is_draw(): return 0",
      "        ",
      "        if is_maximizing:  # AI's turn (MAX)",
      "            best = -math.inf",
      "            for move in self.available_moves():",
      "                self.make_move(move, 'X')",
      "                best = max(best, self.minimax(depth+1, False, alpha, beta))",
      "                self.undo_move(move)",
      "                alpha = max(alpha, best)",
      "                if beta <= alpha:",
      "                    break  # Prune!",
      "            return best",
      "        else:  # Human's turn (MIN)",
      "            best = math.inf",
      "            for move in self.available_moves():",
      "                self.make_move(move, 'O')",
      "                best = min(best, self.minimax(depth+1, True, alpha, beta))",
      "                self.undo_move(move)",
      "                beta = min(beta, best)",
      "                if beta <= alpha:",
      "                    break  # Prune!",
      "            return best",
      "    ",
      "    def get_best_move(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Find the best move for AI using Minimax.\"\"\"",
      "        best_score = -math.inf",
      "        best_move = None",
      "        alpha, beta = -math.inf, math.inf",
      "        ",
      "        for move in self.available_moves():",
      "            self.make_move(move, 'X')",
      "            score = self.minimax(0, False, alpha, beta)",
      "            self.undo_move(move)",
      "            if score > best_score:",
      "                best_score = score",
      "                best_move = move",
      "        ",
      "        return best_move",
      "    ",
      "    def play(self):",
      "        \"\"\"Main game loop.\"\"\"",
      "        print('=' * 40)",
      "        print('🎮 TIC-TAC-TOE: You (O) vs AI (X)')",
      "        print('=' * 40)",
      "        print('Positions: 0-8 (row-wise)')",
      "        ",
      "        while True:",
      "            self.print_board()",
      "            ",
      "            # AI's turn",
      "            if self.current_player == 'X':",
      "                print('AI (X) is thinking...')",
      "                move = self.get_best_move()",
      "                self.make_move(move, 'X')",
      "                print(f'AI placed X at position {move}')",
      "                ",
      "                if self.check_winner('X'):",
      "                    self.print_board()",
      "                    print('🏆 AI wins! Better luck next time!')",
      "                    break",
      "            ",
      "            # Human's turn",
      "            else:",
      "                try:",
      "                    move = int(input('Your turn (O). Enter position (0-8): '))",
      "                    if move not in self.available_moves():",
      "                        print('❌ Invalid move! Try again.')",
      "                        continue",
      "                    self.make_move(move, 'O')",
      "                except (ValueError, IndexError):",
      "                    print('❌ Enter a number between 0-8!')",
      "                    continue",
      "                ",
      "                if self.check_winner('O'):",
      "                    self.print_board()",
      "                    print('🎉 You win! Impressive!')",
      "                    break",
      "            ",
      "            # Check draw",
      "            if self.is_draw():",
      "                self.print_board()",
      "                print('🤝 It\\'s a draw!')",
      "                break",
      "            ",
      "            # Switch player",
      "            self.current_player = 'O' if self.current_player == 'X' else 'X'",
      "",
      "if __name__ == '__main__':",
      "    game = TicTacToe()",
      "    game.play()",
      "$0"
